- `... Kg8`
- `... Kf8`
- `... Kf6` (No, attacked by Rook e7)
- `... Kg6` (No, attacked by Rook e7)

If `e1e6+ Kf7`, `Re7+` is a check.
If `... Kg8`, then `Re8+` is a check!
If `Re8+`, the King must move to `... Kh7` (since g8 is occupied by the Rook? No, the rook is at e8).
So `Re8+ Kh7`.
Then we can play `Re7+` again.

Wait, this is just shuffling.
Is there a way to win the Queen?
The Queen is at f4.
To win the Queen, we need to attack it.
`e1f1` attacks it.
`e1e4` attacks it.
But `e1e6+` is a check.

Let's try `e1e6+`. It's a very active move.
Looking at the options, `e1e6` is likely a strong move in this position.
Let's check if `e1e6+` leads to anything immediate.
`e1e6+`
- `Kf7` -> `Re7+`
- `Kg7` -> `Re7+`
- `Kg5` -> `Re5+` -> `Kg4` -> `Rxe4+` (wins Queen)
- `Kf5` -> `Re5+` -> `Kg4` -> `Rxe4+` (wins Queen)

So if the King moves to the 5th rank, we can potentially win the Queen.
If the King moves to the 7th rank, we can keep checking.

Actually, `e1e6+` is the most forcing.
